   - additional supporting documents (if required by the granting authority).

The new beneficiary will assume the rights and obligations under the Agreement with effect from the
date of accession specified in the accession form (see Annex 3).

ARTICLE 42 -- ASSIGNMENTS OF CLAIMS FOR PAYMENT AGAINST THE
                     GRANTING AUTHORITY

The beneficiaries may not assign any of their claims for payment against the granting authority to
any third party, except if expressly approved in writing by the granting authority on the basis of a
reasoned, written request by the coordinator (on behalf of the beneficiary concerned).

If the granting authority has not accepted the assignment or if the terms of it are not observed, the
assignment will have no effect on it.

In no circumstances will an assignment release the beneficiaries from their obligations towards the
granting authority.

ARTICLE 43 -- APPLICABLE LAW AND SETTLEMENT OF DISPUTES

43.1Applicable law

The Agreement is governed by the applicable EU law, supplemented if necessary by the law of
Belgium.

Special rules may apply for beneficiaries which are international organisations (if any; see Data Sheet,
Point 5).

43.2Dispute settlement

If a dispute concerns the interpretation, application or validity of the Agreement, the parties must bring
action before the EU General Court -- or, on appeal, the EU Court of Justice -- under Article 272
of the Treaty on the Functioning of the EU (TFEU).

For non-EU beneficiaries (if any), such disputes must be brought before the courts of Brussels,
Belgium -- unless an international agreement provides for the enforceability of EU court judgements.

For beneficiaries with arbitration as special dispute settlement forum (if any; see Data Sheet, Point 5),
the dispute will -- in the absence of an amicable settlement -- be settled in accordance with the Rules
for Arbitration published on the Portal.

If a dispute concerns administrative sanctions, offsetting or an enforceable decision under Article 299
TFEU (see Articles 22 and 34), the beneficiaries must bring action before the General Court -- or, on
appeal, the Court of Justice -- under Article 263 TFEU.

For grants where the granting authority is an EU executive agency (see Preamble), actions against
offsetting and enforceable decisions must be brought against the European Commission (not against
the granting authority; see also Article 22).

PROJECT SUMMARY

Project summary

Grant Preparation (General Information screen) -- Provide an overall description of your project (including context and overall
objectives, planned activities and main achievements, and expected results and impacts (on target groups, change procedures,
capacities, innovation etc)). This summary should give readers a clear idea of what your project is about.

Use the project summary from your proposal.

U_CAN facilitates a practical bottom-up approach with Ukrainian cities towards climate neutrality. Following the
European standards and the European Green Deal (EGD), the project starts with a core group of 8 Ukrainian cities,
Dresden as a Mission City, NGOs and research institutions. This networking and co-creation process addresses
the current challenges of the Ukrainian cities and their key sectors. Within 48 months, the CNSC-UA twinning
partners exchange experiences with further network municipalities throughout the country, adapting to European
climate standards with experts like DIN and transitioning together. The core stakeholders work closely with their
city administration in building capacities in the key sectors identified. In light of the war, co-creation and Citizen
Science augmented training activities ensure an effective, value-based and needs-oriented approach. Communication
and dissemination by EU partners near the UA borders enable - besides an online platform - the personal exchange of
experiences with the CNSC twin cities. This step-by-step synergy building and facilitation includes vision workshops,
road mapping and stakeholder co-creation to twin European and Ukrainian cities. This leads to use case development
in each pilot city supporting pilot implementation of measures for climate mitigation and adaptation. This is combined
with an extensive plan for dissemination, exploitation and communication led by European and Ukrainian umbrella
organisations, standardisation initiatives, and local and national representatives. These efforts are assisted through
functioning and already working city partnerships, seamlessly integrating the Ukrainian Cities into the vision of the
EGD. The given diversity in geopolitical characteristics ensures the involvement of all relevant sectors and the equal
distribution of support, ranging from smaller cities to the Ukrainian capital and most important lighthouse City Kyiv.

Work package WP1 - Project Management

Work Package Number WP1                Lead Beneficiary  1 - TUD

Work Package Name  Project Management

Start Month                            1 End Month                                                         48

Objectives

The aim of this WP is to ensure effective management of the project with emphasis on meeting the European
Commission requirements. The objectives are:
-Overall management and monitoring of project progress of WPs against deliverables;
-Ensuring accurate and timely reporting on activities and communication with the EC;
-Ensuring Compliance and the fulfilment of ethical necessities; and
-Exchange with Network Partners.

Description

Task 1.1: Management of Consortium and Work Packages
M1-M48; TUD (WP-Leads, all partners)
The Coordinator, TUD, will coordinate activities and periodic technical reporting using an efficient management
structure. TUD signs responsible for coordination, communication and regular interaction with WP Leads to monitor
the work progress and the external representation of the Project consortium towards the EU and stakeholders. The WP
Leads monitor the advances in each WP. They are the first supervisory unit for risk management, ensuring that the
time-schedule is maintained, indicating deviations, consolidating partner information, and monitoring milestones and
deliverables. Supervision of project progress focusing on achieving the project objectives will be carried out on the basis
of the Project Management Handbook (D1.1) to monitor work flow within and across work packages; to assess and
review deliverables and milestones; to ensure knowledge transfer and communication among work packages, to manage
risks and conflicts and prepare meetings.

Task 1.2: Administration and Financial Management
M1-M48; TUD-EPC (all partners)
The European Project Center of the TUD will support U_CAN with legal, financial and administrative management
of the project and, through their experience, make sure that all EC reporting requirements are met in time and with
the expected quality. This will include contract (change) management of Grant and Consortium Agreements, financial
management (budget monitoring, financial reporting, distribution of funding), administrative issues (mailing lists,
document repository, meeting organisation) and maintaining a clear channel of communication between the consortium
and the EC. The Project Management Handbook will guide the management of U_CAN.

Task 1.3: Data Management, Ethics and Gender Assurance
M1-M48; TUD (ULA, all partners)
TUD will ensure, that the procedures and policies governing project management (Quality Management, Internal
Information Flow, Dissemination, Consortium Agreement, Knowledge and Intellectual Property, Ethical Issues, Gender
Aspects, Conflict Resolution, Risk assessment) will be followed. A Data Management Plan (D1.2) will be drafted
according to FAIR principles and will be reviewed with all project partners at the beginning and before the end-of-
project, based on the involved data collection activities of the project.

Task 1.4: Working Network Board formation and operation
M1-M48; TUD (WUST, all partners)
With a Coordination and Support Action approach, U_CAN aims to have an active interdisciplinary network of partners
who have the expertise and interest to support the project implementation. Such partners come from different domains
(CNSC Mission cities, Ukrainian cities, Association "Energy Efficient Cities of Ukraine" (EECU), Ministries and GIZ-
Ukraine) and have communicated their interests stating their relevance and role in U_CAN. The exchange with the
proposed Working Network Partners Board (M01) is planned within the proposed meetings and other project events
and is managed by TUD. Up to 20 members will represent public administrations (cities/regions) and policy makers,
academia, industry and members of civil society for which some travel budget is foreseen.

                                                                                                           8
Project: 101148374 -- U_CAN -- HORIZON-MISS-2023-CIT-02

                                                         Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)3060237 - 25/04/2024

Work package WP2 - Creation of U_CAN project platform for knowledge sharing and
networking

Work Package Number WP2  Lead Beneficiary                       13 - OMB

Work Package Name  Creation of U_CAN project platform for knowledge sharing and networking

Start Month              1 End Month                                                        48

Objectives

The aim of this WP is to establish and operate a project platform for data and knowledge sharing, and for stakeholder
networking. The objective is:
-Establish a project platform for collaboration, engagement of stakeholder network and knowledge sharing among
Ukrainian cities that are committed to the goal of carbon neutrality.

Description

Task 2.1: U_CAN project platform creation
M1-M6; OMB (TPF, ECSA, ENV, all partners)
The U_CAN project platform (M03) will be created as an active online platform for engagement of stakeholder networks,
integrating project activities and findings. It will also function as a knowledge dissemination platform by accumulating
and sharing best practices accumulated during the project duration. It will also publish and provide educational materials
as a part of educating and disseminating knowledge to a wider audience. After the end of the project, the platform will
continue operating as a centre for urban climate neutrality popularisation, engagement of other cities in Ukraine into
the transition to climate neutrality, sustaining and creating collaborations between the cities and EU and UN climate
goals entities. The technical implementation of the platform will be developed by TPF, while structure and content
development, as well as general task management will be carried out by OMB with the support of all project partners.
The platform will be hosted in the EU according to GDPR and other legal requirements.

Task 2.2: Interactive Mapping tool
M7-M48; TPF (OMB, MCU, WUST)
A multipurpose Interactive Map (D2.1) will be developed to serve two needs. Firstly, the tool will map various project
and network partners to showcase the reach of the U_CAN stakeholder network. Secondly, it will accumulate and store
various data to be used in U_CAN analytical studies as well as data and findings generated by the project e.g. within
WP3, WP4, WP5, WP6 and WP7. Thus, the map will serve as an instrument for dissemination of project results. The map
will be created based on an openly-available Business Intelligence & Data Analytics platform to ensure easy navigation
and re-use of U_CAN data. It will be continuously updated through the project implementation period to integrate new
data and results.

Work package WP3 - CNSC synergy building towards climate goals

Work Package Number WP3  Lead Beneficiary                       8 - WUST

Work Package Name  CNSC synergy building towards climate goals

Start Month              1 End Month                                                        48

Objectives

The aim of this WP is to initiate and conduct twinning among the CNSC mission cities and other EU entities with
collaboration-minded Ukrainian stakeholders involved in localised climate action initiatives. The objectives are:
-Develop consensus building in envisioning climate goals in various planning and reconstruction sectors in Ukraine
and initiate teaming and partnerships between Ukrainian and CNSC Mission cities;
-Develop pilot Use cases for the Ukrainian cities of Lviv, Kyiv, Zhytomyr, Khmelnytskyi, Ivano Frankivsk and Vinnytsia
that aspire climate neutrality;
-Support local authorities of pilot cities in building capacities for climate policy dialogues and creating opportunities
for consensus-building with local communities and other stakeholder groups, including determining the economic and
social benefits of the green transformation for all potential stakeholders; and

                                                                                            9
Project: 101148374 -- U_CAN -- HORIZON-MISS-2023-CIT-02

                                                            Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)3060237 - 25/04/2024

-Collaborate with the Mission platform and identify online networking opportunities for collaboration with funding
institutes, and additional stakeholders in interested Ukrainian cities with other EU organisations by network building to
link Ukrainian cities with EU and UN climate goals.

Description

Task 3.1: Identification of key sectors in climate actions
M4-M12; AU (ISKPI, WUST, ENV, JRG, SFPA)
This task involves identification of crucial Sectors for climate action in Ukraine (D3.1) that in the long-term can be
addressed in the war-affected regions and entire country. AU together with ENV will analyse the pre-war and postwar
environmental issues, the geopolitical situation in Ukraine and the pilot cities. It will focus also on the interconnections
between key sectors, stakeholders and institutional capacities in the pilot cities of Ukraine. It will review the public
policy frameworks and climate action plans in selected pilot cities regions and the results of the analysis. D3.1 will
provide T3.2 the grounds for twinning exchanges with CNSC mission cities. Sectoral assessment will be based on best
practices at EU level and internationally (e.g., EU Strategies, 6th IPCC Assessment Report, IFIs Guidance Documents).
ISKPI will conduct a Territory Suitability Assessment to recommend Suitability for Solar Power Station Placement in
Ukraine (D3.2).

Task 3.2: CNSC and Ukraine twinning initiation
M7-M30; WUST (LHD, TUD, TPF, all pilot cities in UA)
This task will introduce synergies between partner CNSC mission cities and the six pilot cities in Ukraine (M03) by
designing and executing a series of tailor-made knowledge and experience exchange sessions (KEES) (D3.3) building
on the findings of T3.1 and the goals of T3.3 and T3.4. It will develop a detailed programme and relations between the
series of events and key areas requiring synergies.

Task 3.3: Pilot use cases conception
M7-24; AU (WUST, LHD, TUD, TPF, AU, all pilot cities in UA)
This task will be based on T3.2 to create city-specific scenarios (use cases) with the aim to identify possible transition
pathways, government actions, stakeholder contributions, and engagement activities in 5 Ukrainian cities. It will assess
the scope for localised energy transition and climate action initiatives with emphasis on institutional approaches and
create a catalogue of best practices relevant to the Pilot use cases (D3.4). The pilot activities will be in line with the EGD
and will depend on the characteristics and needs of the different Ukrainian partner cities.

Task 3.4: Engagement with CNSC Mission Platform
M1-48; WUST (LHD, TPF, TUD, ENV)
This task will coordinate with the CNSC Mission platform (M04) and other non-project organisations like Creating
Actionable Futures (CrAFt), Eurocities, ICLEI Local Governments for Sustainability, U_LEAD etc., to highlight and
promote U_CAN activities on different platforms and the NEB Initiative.

Work package WP4 - Pilot Implementation in Ukraine, supported by European front-
runners

Work Package Number WP4                                     Lead Beneficiary  16 - CIL

Work Package Name  Pilot Implementation in Ukraine, supported by European front-runners

Start Month              13 End Month                                                    45

Objectives

The aim of this WP is to implement short to medium-term climate mitigation strategies in 6 pilot cities in Ukraine (Lviv,
Kyiv, Zhytomyr, Khmelnytskyi, Ivano Frankivsk and Vinnytsia). The objectives are:
-Support implementation of pilot activities in six cities to demonstrate climate-neutral city solutions; and
-Integrate learnings from project and contemporary practices in short and medium-term strategies of local governing
bodies in Ukraine.

Description
Task 4.1: Assessment of climate indicators in pilot cities

                                                                                         10
Project: 101148374 -- U_CAN -- HORIZON-MISS-2023-CIT-02

                                                         Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)3060237 - 25/04/2024

M13-M21; OMB (ENV, BRIT, all pilot partners)
This task will develop and deploy a methodology for data requirement to assess climate neutrality and impact indicators
(D4.1) in pilot cities. It will be conducted in coordination with partners from pilot cities. Climate indicators will be
collected, organised and assessed to indicate key hazards and potential risks in the pilots (M05).

Task 4.2: Climate-neutral pilot activities in Lviv
M13-M42; CIL (OMB, ENV)
CIL will implement two activities for the city of Lviv: (i) development of the Positive Energy District (PED) features
and (ii) setting up a Bureau for EU Integration.
(i)CIL will develop positive energy district technologies through integrating energy efficient solutions, Renewable
Energy Sources (RES) and smart energy management in a pilot neighbourhood. The climate-neutral solutions and
approach from pilot activities will be included in an Implementation Plan for Lviv (D4.2) to cater to the scope of scale-
up and replication.
(ii)The City of Lviv is setting up a Bureau for EU Integration and an Office of Lviv in Brussels (M07) to institutionalize
EU integration processes in general and synchronize EU strategies, policies and other governmental practices on the
municipal level.

Task 4.3: Climate-neutral pilot activities in Kyiv
M13-M42; ISKPI (OMB, ENV)
ISKPI will develop recommendations for the use of underground space of Kyiv for systemic influence on the reduction
of greenhouse gas emissions produced by transport. The climate-neutral approach from the pilot activity will be included
in a general Implementation Plan for Kyiv (D4.3) to cater to the scope of replication.
A systematic assessment of the reduction of automobile emissions when moving traffic flows underground will be carried
out (ISKPI EE). The Urban Atlas for Kyiv for 2020-2022 and maps of changes in land use, pollution and temperature
fields of the districts of the city of Kyiv will be created based on the database of satellite observations (ISKPI MM).
The author's system methodology and tools for making planning decisions for the development of underground transport
infrastructure for the tasks of climate neutrality of the city will be developed and implemented (ISKPI EE). Based on it,
an analysis of routing and mitigating the risks of the transport infrastructure of the city of Kyiv when using the system of
car tunnels and subway lines will be carried out, in particular, the ranking and identification of the priority (feasibility)
of the construction of the planned tunnels in the near and medium term from the standpoint of climate neutrality (ISKPI
EE). Coordination and implementation of the developed recommendations (ISKPI EE).

Task 4.4: Climate-neutral pilot activities in Zhytomyr
M13-M42; ZCC (OMB, ENV)
ZCC will conduct two activities in the city of Zhytomyr:
(i)development of a floating solar power plant - The pilot aims at achieving the strategic goal of the city `Achieving
European energy efficiency and energy saving standards', as well as operational tasks of stimulation of the development
and use of renewable energy sources and the use of renewable energy sources in the field of heat supply and power
generation, which are defined by the city development strategy; and
(ii)development of urban gardening models based on the former municipal plant nurseries. - The pilot aims to promote
urban gardening as a practice among citizens and people who are interested in farming, increase green areas in the city
used for urban gardening and increase the production of local food within the Zhytomyr Community.
The climate-neutral solutions and approach from pilot activities will be included in an Implementation Plan for Zhytomyr
(D4.4) to cater to the scope of scale-up and replication.

Task 4.5: Climate-neutral pilot activities in Khmelnytskyi
M13-M42; KhNU (LYKUML, OMB, ENV, LHD)
KhNU and LYKUML along with the Khmelnytskyi City Council will conduct two activities in the city of Khmelnytskyi.
The first is concerned with dissemination activities for knowledge sharing related to CO2 emissions reduction in cities
that are working towards climate neutrality including a conference (M06) to bring together multiple Ukrainian cities and
key stakeholders involved in the transport sector; and one dedicated workshop to discuss efficient urban traffic systems.
The second is the development of a centre for the support of local policies in the field of climate-neutral construction
and energy. The climate-neutral findings and approach from these activities will be included in an Implementation Plan
for Khmelnytskyi (D4.5) to cater to the scope of replication.

Task 4.6: Climate-neutral pilot activities in Ivano-Frankivsk
M13-M42; BRIT (OMB, ENV)
BRIT in close cooperation with Ivano-Frankivsk city council will implement a photovoltaic installation within the public
building in central Ivano-Frankivsk. The building will be selected based on the needs assessment and with consideration

                                                         11
Project: 101148374 -- U_CAN -- HORIZON-MISS-2023-CIT-02

                                                         Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)3060237 - 25/04/2024

of dissemination possibilities, other project and synergetic activities. The installation will consist of a PV-panel solution,
smart metering, LED-lighting and public availability.
The climate-neutral solution and approach from the pilot activity will be included in an Implementation Plan for Ivano-
Frankivsk (D4.6) to cater to the scope of scale-up and replication possibilities. The Implementation Plan for Ivano-
Frankivsk will be aligned with the Ivano-Frankivsk municipal green deal and Sustainable energy and climate action plan
till 2030 and will be agreed with the Ivano-Frankivsk city council.

Task 4.8: Performance assessment of initiatives in six pilot cities
M22-M45; ENV (OMB, BRIT, all pilot partners)
During this task ENV will evaluate the impact and performance of the pilot activities based on the framework developed
in T4.1. It is deemed necessary to create a roadmap and prioritize measures for implementation. It will, where relevant,
include energy audits, risk assessment analysis and summarize the project's contribution to climate mitigation based on
the pilot results in the form of a Performance Assessment Report (D4.8). It will assist local governing bodies in making
strategic decisions towards climate neutrality. Based on the performance assessment, it will also develop a framework
for replication and scaling up of pilot initiatives in other parts of Ukraine.

Work package WP5 - Modernization and standardisation of technologies and practices

Work Package Number WP5  Lead Beneficiary                10 - ULA

Work Package Name  Modernization and standardisation of technologies and practices

Start Month              4 End Month                                                                                45

Objectives

The aim of this WP is to support the process of standardisation and modernisation of technologies. Objectives are:
-Standardisation activities relevant to the EU and international levels;
-Modernisation of green technologies in the building sector;
-Procedures and protocols development for building stocks and its reconstruction; and
-Citizen participation and engagement in local climate adaptation strategies.

Description

Task 5.1: Standardisation activities
M4-M45; DIN (JRG, ULA, TUD, all pilot partners)
This task will investigate published standards and standards under development, as well as relevant technical committees
at EU and international level allowing to consolidate existing standards and build up on them (D5.1). The investigation
will build on the existing cooperation with Ukraine and compare the standardization needs and opportunities identified
by the project partners. If suitable project results are identified, DIN is going to apply for a public European Committee
for Standardisation (CEN) Workshop to create a CEN Workshop Agreement or contribute to ongoing standardisation
activities.
Close cooperation and interaction with the other work packages ensures that the project partners are made aware of the
existing standardization landscape and that the identification of standardization potentials is supported.

Task 5.2: Modernisation of technologies for decarbonisation of industries
M10-M33; JRG (AU, ULA, OMB)
This task will develop guidelines for modernisation of technologies towards decarbonisation of industries (D5.2) and
produce recommendations for cities interested in transitioning to clean energy. It will also develop a framework for the
adaptation of nature-based solutions. This task will also consult decision-makers on questions of quality assurance for
the expansion of renewable energy in cities in Ukraine and assess the risks and quality of energy projects. It will also
create a roadmap for decarbonisation of industries (M08).

Task 5.3: Procedures for repurposing war-affected demolition materials
M10-M33; ULA (RCSD, OMB, ENV)
This task will develop procedures for recycling of secondary raw materials (D5.3) and the reuse/repurpose of building
components coming from demolitions. It will do so by conducting preliminary studies on the quality and quantity of
secondary raw materials and building components, the census of building technologies and typologies applied in the
Ukrainian cities to evaluate the main construction features and the bill of quantities of building stock and develop specific

                                                                                                                    12
Project: 101148374 -- U_CAN -- HORIZON-MISS-2023-CIT-02

                                                         Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)3060237 - 25/04/2024

tools for its agile assessment. The deliverable will take into consideration various aspects including environmental
considerations, technical feasibility, economic viability and regulatory compliance. These studies and tools will lead to
the organisation of protocols and guidelines that could assist local institutions in the management of building rubble and
the reuse/repurpose of building components.

Task 5.4: Protocols for renovation/energy retrofit of existing buildings
M10-M36; ULA (OMB, RCSD)
This task will conduct thermal analysis of building stock to conceive decision-making tools for a multi-scenario design
approach (D5.4) addressed to building reconstruction or refurbishment for the adaptive reuse of existing buildings from
a climate perspective. It will contribute to a multidisciplinary design approach compliant with local climate features
that support development of guidelines regarding energy efficiency of existing building stock, particularly focusing on
building envelopes.

Task 5.5: Citizen participation in local climate governance
M13-M42; TUD (ECSA, SFPA, ENV, WUST, AU, ULA, UA-partners)
The task involves conducting a comprehensive study to explore citizen participation mechanisms in local climate
governance within the country. The primary objective is to analyse existing practices, identify barriers, and propose
recommendations for enhancing citizen engagement in climate-related decision-making processes at the local level.
The deliverable for this task will be a detailed report that includes findings, policy recommendations, and a toolkit for
local authorities to improve citizen participation in climate governance (D5.5), which will be disseminated in a webinar
to stakeholders of Ukrainian partner cities (M09).

Work package WP6 - Capacity building and localised knowledge mobilisation in Ukraine

Work Package Number WP6  Lead Beneficiary                14 - ISKPI

Work Package Name  Capacity building and localised knowledge mobilisation in Ukraine

Start Month              18 End Month                                                 45

Objectives

The aim of this WP is to conduct training, technical capacity building for various target groups and conduct local
exchanges in Ukraine. The objectives are:
-Technical capacity building via development of ad-hoc training programmes; and
-Localised multi-sectoral exchanges in Ukraine.

Description

Task 6.1: Training and capacity-building
M25-M36; ISKPI (TPF, ECSA, DIN, ENV, WUST, MCU)
This task will develop a 'Climate neutrality of cities' Guide (D6.1) to share findings from WP3, WP4 and WP5. The
course will be targeted towards the city officials, planners, Masters students and relevant stakeholders. The Guide will
disseminate context-specific materials in both offline and online formats and will be shared firstly with the network
partners in Ukraine and will be promoted by other Ukrainian partners in their network of cities. The Guide will
incorporate comprehensive understanding and practical application of the proposed methodologies for climate neutrality
of cities and forward-looking decision support tools. ISKPI will also develop and conduct a webinar on `Decarbonisation
of energy sector' (D6.2). The content of which will be posted on the U_CAN project platform and on other pre-existing
educational, online distribution platforms. ISKPI will also integrate the theme of climate neutrality of cities in their
university curriculum.

Task 6.2: Climate Briefing for Mayors and city representatives of Ukraine
M18-M33; MCU (TUD, TPF)
This task will develop and promote leadership programs `Mayors' briefing' targeting Mayors, Heads of environmental
protection departments, and interested public organisations in Ukraine (D6.3). The programs will make emphasis on
strategic-level actions influencing decision making processes, while recognising the need to involve other relevant actors
in the formulation and implementation of concrete actions at operational levels.

Task 6.3: Localised pilot-related knowledge mobilisation

                                                                                      13
Project: 101148374 -- U_CAN -- HORIZON-MISS-2023-CIT-02

                                                         Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)3060237 - 25/04/2024

M34-M45; ZCC (all pilot cities, TPF, BRIT)
This task will facilitate mobilisation of knowledge implemented in pilot activities among all pilot partners and other
network partners in Ukraine based on site-visits to pilot cities (D6.4). This will enable localised and context-based sharing
of pilot findings, strategies, and lessons learned and will potentially foster further collaborations and crosslearning
to accelerate the adoption of climate-neutral practices across various Ukrainian cities.

Task 6.4: Political dialogues with Ukrainian decision-makers
M24-M42; ISKPI (TUD, TPF, MCU)
This task will engage the Mayor of Kyiv, decision makers, Ministries and relevant stakeholders in political dialogues. It
will build on the success stories from the pilot activities and also promote applicable findings from WP5 for adaptation
of standards, green technologies and industrial decarbonisation for appropriate adaptation and replication of results. The
task will include at least one political dialogue event in Ukraine supported by online participation of European front-
runners to influence policy discussions and drive further collaborations between CNSC mission cities and Ukrainian
decision makers and one round-table discussion in Kyiv (M10).

Work package WP7 - Communication, dissemination and exploitation

Work Package Number WP7  Lead Beneficiary                         3 - ECSA

Work Package Name  Communication, dissemination and exploitation

Start Month              1 End Month                                        48

Objectives

The aim of this WP is to ensure widespread communication, dissemination, and exploitation of U_CAN findings among
the European and Ukrainian stakeholders and support the realisation of the EGD. It will also foster new collaborations
towards climate-neutral practices in Ukrainian cities. The objectives are:
-Communication of project activities, findings and events via the U_CAN project platform;
-Dissemination of project results, success stories and knowledge on various platforms; and
-Exploitation of project results to foster further initiatives and collaborations.

Description

Task 7.1: U_CAN communication and dissemination activities
M1-M48; ECSA (SFPA, all partners)
ECSA will collaborate with all project partners to draft a Dissemination, exploitation and communication plan (D7.1). A
suite of communication channels will be established to ensure regular updates about project progress and achievements
for identified stakeholders and broader interest communities. The final set of communication channels will be determined
based on user and stakeholder profiles, optimised to meet their needs. These channels will encompass the U_CAN
project platform, social media accounts, a project newsletter with 1-2 yearly editions, a project brochure outlining the
project's primary aims, and a minimum of two project videos introducing the project and providing progress updates.
In parallel with these communication channels, the project and CNSC teaming success stories and findings will be
disseminated through a range of means, participation in international conferences and events, and publications in peer-
reviewed journals. In close collaboration with all project partners (in particular SFPA for exploitation), adjustments will
be made to the dissemination, exploitation and communication plan as necessary, capitalising on effective approaches
and addressing any shortcomings. The updates of this plan will be documented in Deliverable D7.2, D7.3 and D7.8.
ECSA will facilitate the sharing of lessons learned and best practices to integrate climate-neutral approaches in Ukrainian
cities along with CNSC partners in various online and hybrid dissemination activities. The activities will also use digital
platforms to create a virtual community for disseminating knowledge about climate neutrality.
A communication team will be established with one representative per partner, coordinated by ECSA, which will meet
regularly. Internal communication activities will include monthly meetings within individual Work Packages and a
minimum of one quarterly consortium meeting.

U_CAN will also contribute, upon invitation by the CINEA, to common information and dissemination activities
to increase the visibility and synergies between HE/H2020 supported actions. U_CAN is committed to establishing
a cooperation with the projects funded under the same topic HORIZON-MISS-2023-CIT-02-01 as part of cluster
Associating Ukrainian cities to the Cities Mission as well as with the Climate-neutral and Smart Cities Mission Platform.
This cooperation shall materialise in the coordination of monitoring, communication and dissemination activities of

                                                                            14
Project: 101148374 -- U_CAN -- HORIZON-MISS-2023-CIT-02

                                                         Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)3060237 - 25/04/2024

mutual interest, and any other type of collaborations that may occur during the conduct of this Coordination and Support
Action project, on the basis of mutual benefit.
U_CAN has flexibility in identifying activities and means of collaboration with the other projects funded under the
same topic and the Mission Platform. The collaboration could cover e.g. defining complementary solutions, measures
or methodologies, capacity building, networking or dissemination activities (such as webinars, workshops, site visits or
publications). A detailed description of the specific activities and common actions that will be undertaken will be further
defined during the first months of the project lifetime.
To foster complementarity and avoid duplication, all the projects funded under topic HORIZON-MISS-2023-CIT-02-01
and part of cluster Associating Ukrainian cities to the Cities Mission are expected to share information on their
coordination and support activities and relevant results to the Mission Platform, including, where possible, the provision
of data to enable the monitoring of progress towards the achievement of the Climate-neutral and Smart Cities Mission
objectives. The collaboration with the Mission Platform will be formalised through a Memorandum of Understanding
to be concluded as soon as possible after the project starting date.

Task 7.2: Consolidation of U_CAN success stories
M22-M45; TUD (pilot cities, all partners)
This task will consolidate highlights of project knowledge such as success stories from pilot implementations and best
practices into a U_CAN Highlights report (D7.4). The material preparation will be led by TUD and be supported by
the research partners with aims to contribute publications for the scientific community, attend conferences, prepare
presentations and start other initiatives for practitioner events. The project's Highlights report will be circulated via
various dissemination channels, events and among the scientific community in the form of derived peer-reviewed journal
articles, poster and oral presentations at national and international conferences. It will be accessible on the project
website. Peer-reviewed scientific journal publications will be open access.

Task 7.3: Exchanges with Mayors of Europe and Global Covenant of Mayors
M31-M45; MCU (CIL, SFPA, LHD, TPF)
MCU will exchange with the Mayors of Europe to facilitate peer to peer cooperation between cities and regions in
the European Union and in Ukraine (M11). Based on past cooperations, it will also engage with the Global Covenant
of Mayors as well as with the European Alliance of Cities and Regions towards carbon neutral reconstruction of
Ukraine. The engagement will be based on at least two events and will promote cross-border exchange of experiences,
solutions, and expertise fostering new collaboration in the implementation of climate-neutral strategies. The climate
actions initiatives within the U_CAN project will also be shared within the international investment roadshow `Rebirth
of the Nation' that aims to popularise climate mitigation awareness.

Task 7.4: Annual Central European Energy Conference
M22-M48; SFPA (RCSD, ECSA, all partners) SFPA will include a U_CAN panel in its annual Central European Energy
Conference (M12) in at least two years as a part of disseminating project results and exploiting them based on the
dialogues with various Ukrainian and EU participants. SFPA will create an exploitation plan (included in D7.1, D7.2,
D7.3 and D7.8) to further the outcomes of Pilot cities to industries, public authorities, researchers, policy makers and
civil society among Ukrainian and EU stakeholders and it will work with RCSD and ECSA to develop exploitation
actions. SFPA will also conduct an exchange with Ukrainian stakeholders (D7.7), which are intended to take place in
Czechia and Slovakia.

Task 7.5: Initiation of small-scale projects with donor funding
M31-M48; RCSD (SFPA, JRG)
This task will coordinate and promote project exploitation activities, including initiating small-scale projects based on
a framework developed for modernisation of technologies and replicate them in transitioning Ukraine's heatgenerating
sector to biofuels by engaging donor funding. The initiation of the small-scale projects will be based on the evaluation
of their central heating systems and creation of a roadmap for Ukraine's heat generating sector (D7.5) for the selected
communities, outlining the pathway to modernise their
respective centralized heating infrastructures. The task will also curate an extensive repository as a part of the roadmap
with information pertinent to potential donors, investors, and financial entities. Some of them are WB, EBRD who are
capable of financing projects aimed at the green transition. These preparatory steps will culminate in the submission of
project proposals to potential donors, international financial organizations, and prospective investors. The anticipated
outcomes of this endeavour are multifaceted and encompass both tangible outputs and overarching implications for
municipalities and stakeholders involved in fostering the multiplication of U_CAN results. With a focus on tangible
U_CAN actions, the selected task will contribute to the strategic approach aligning with the exploitation ambition of
fostering EGD ambitions in the district heating landscape in Ukraine.

Task 7.6: Roadmap to empower climate action collaboration

                                                         15
Project: 101148374 -- U_CAN -- HORIZON-MISS-2023-CIT-02

                                                         Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)3060237 - 25/04/2024

M34-M48; ECSA (JRG, CIL, AU, TPF, ENV)
A roadmap will be created for empowering citizens, civil society organisations and other relevant actors to guide them
in the process of collectively developing effective strategies to address climate action challenges. For this purpose,
the feedback from pilot activities in WP4 and T5.5 will be used together with lessons learned in previous experiences
in citizen and stakeholder engagement in climate action. The roadmap will include the quadruple dimensions of
context, people, process and impact. The `context' dimension will explore the surrounding circumstances for industrial
decarbonisation in the selected Ukrainian and EU cities. The `people' dimension will emphasise on the mobilisation of
the relevant individuals and organizations. The `process' dimension will concentrate on operational and strategic factors
that accelerate industrial decarbonisation. Lastly, the `impact' dimension will assess the transformative and sustainable
effects of industrial decarbonisation and other green transformation initiatives. The roadmap to empower climate action
collaboration (D7.6) will be a dynamic and a strategic guide, facilitating the integration of various perspectives, expertise,
and insights into the decision-making processes related to climate adaptation.

Deliverable D3.1 - Base report: Sectors for Climate Actions in Ukraine

Deliverable Number  D3.1                    Lead Beneficiary             9 - AU
Deliverable Name
Type                Base report: Sectors for Climate Actions in Ukraine  PU - Public
Due Date (month)                                                         WP3
                    R -- Document, report   Dissemination Level

                                            12 Work Package No

Description

This report is intended to pinpoint significant political, economic, and social sectors that require climate action in Ukraine
and evaluate the local circumstances for executing the same in the pilot cities. The report will be based on a review
of national and local policy documents as well as climate action plans to determine the course of Ukrainian climate
governance. It will identify key stakeholders and institutional capabilities within the pilot cities and draw on secondary
climate governance research and international best practice documents, such as EU Strategies, IPCC Assessment Reports,
and IFI's guidance documents, to identify implementation gaps and opportunities for future climate actions.

Project: 101148374 -- U_CAN -- HORIZON-MISS-2023-CIT-02

                                                         Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)3060237 - 25/04/2024

Deliverable D3.2 - Ukrainian Territory Suitability for Solar Power Station Placement

Deliverable Number  D3.2                       Lead Beneficiary         14 - ISKPI
Deliverable Name
Type                Ukrainian Territory Suitability for Solar Power Station Placement
Due Date (month)
                    R -- Document, report      Dissemination Level      PU - Public

                                               12 Work Package No       WP3

Description

The aim of this document is to provide a high-resolution land suitability map for solar PV installations in Ukraine's
Solar Power Station, which will identify suitable regions and locations for placing solar farms based on climatic and
landscape data to maximise energy potential. The data will be presented on an interactive dashboard, which will show
the suitability map, solar radiation, climate, and terrain data. Additionally, the document includes a methodology report,
which describes how the spatial data from multiple sources was analysed using GIS, fuzzy logic, and multi-criteria
techniques to determine suitability.

Deliverable D3.3 - KEES (Knowledge and Experience Exchange Sessions) reports

Deliverable Number  D3.3                       Lead Beneficiary         8 - WUST
Deliverable Name
Type                KEES (Knowledge and Experience Exchange Sessions) reports
Due Date (month)
                    R -- Document, report      Dissemination Level      SEN - Sensitive

                                               30 Work Package No       WP3

Description

The KEES Report provides a record of interactions and synergies among stakeholders involved in the U_CAN project
(including twinning activities). It will outline the purpose, progress, main outcomes, and key lessons learned from the
sessions. The report will also explain the schedule, methodology used, and specific knowledge transfer goals for each
(online, hybrid or in-person) event. Additionally, it will include needs assessments and summarised results of surveys
(ex-ante and ex-post) conducted among KEES participants throughout the process.

Deliverable D4.8 - Climate Impact and Performance Assessment Report for the pilot Cities

Deliverable Number  D4.8                   Lead Beneficiary     6 - ENV
Deliverable Name
Type                Climate Impact and Performance Assessment Report for the pilot Cities
Due Date (month)
                    R -- Document, report  Dissemination Level  PU - Public

                                           42 Work Package No   WP4

Description

Deliverable D4.8 is the performance assessment report of all activities designed and implemented in the pilot cities
between months 13-36. The roadmap design and the measures prioritization will be developed using data and indicators
generated from the previous activities and following a methodological approach and the degree of climate neutrality and
impact of climate indicators in the different cities will be assessed.
The roadmap will be designed to at least assess:
-The effectiveness degree of each measure / activity through different KPIs
-The capacity of the implementation bodies, the needs and the difficulties experienced
-The financing methods and funding sources used
It will also provide the framework for the future development of similar activities and projects in other Oblasts.

Deliverable D5.2 - Guidelines for modernization of technologies towards decarbonisation of
industries

Deliverable Number  D5.2                   Lead Beneficiary     11 - JRG
Deliverable Name
Type                Guidelines for modernization of technologies towards decarbonisation of industries
Due Date (month)
                    R -- Document, report  Dissemination Level  PU - Public

                                           24 Work Package No   WP5

Description

The guidelines will contain the following:
a)Description of the most important climate-neutral technologies for construction, energy supply and the production
of industrial goods;
b)Comparison of EU and Ukrainian guidelines and legal regulations for decarbonization of industry;
c)Analysis of different industrial sectors according to their influence on climate change in Ukrainian pilot cities;
d)Determination of the need for green transformation in the industry of the Ukrainian pilot cities: organizational,
methodological and financial aspects;

                                                                                                        28
Project: 101148374 -- U_CAN -- HORIZON-MISS-2023-CIT-02

e)Justification of nature-based solutions for new construction and modernization of industrial facilities and residential
buildings;
f)Justification of the necessary resources for the industry's transition to climate neutrality: creation of different scenarios;
g)Potential and limitations for green industrial transformation in Ukraine;
h)Development of criteria for selecting climate-neutral technologies in industry;
i)Measures to increase energy efficiency in Ukraine's industry;
j)Planning projects to increase energy efficiency in Ukraine's industry;
k)Planning the use of innovative recycling technologies in resource-intensive industrial sectors of the Ukrainian pilot
cities.

Deliverable D5.4 - Tool for the implementation of renewables on urban and building scale,
also enhancing environmental sustainability

Deliverable Number  D5.4   Lead Beneficiary              10 - ULA
Deliverable Name
                    Tool for the implementation of renewables on urban and building scale, also enhancing
Type                environmental sustainability
Due Date (month)
                    OTHER  Dissemination Level           PU - Public

                           36 Work Package No            WP5

Description

a.Agile assessment of thermal analysis of building stock.
b.Decision-making tools for a multi-scenario design approach for the assessment of the options of building

d.Guidelines for the energy efficiency of existing building stocks, particularly focusing on building envelope.

f.Strategies for urban planning oriented to the mitigation of the vulnerability and natural hazards of the city.
g.Strategies for the improvement of urban service infrastructures through the design of smart cities' devices and their
management protocols.

Deliverable D6.1 - 'Climate neutrality of cities in Ukraine' Guide

Deliverable Number  D6.1                    Lead Beneficiary         14 - ISKPI
Deliverable Name
Type                'Climate neutrality of cities in Ukraine' Guide  PU - Public
Due Date (month)                                                     WP6
                    R -- Document, report   Dissemination Level

                                            33 Work Package No

Description

The 'Climate Neutrality of Cities in Ukraine' Guide' will be targeted towards the city officials, planners, masters students
and relevant stakeholders. The content will focus on future-oriented methodologies and decision support tools, providing
a comprehensive understanding and practical application of achieving climate neutrality in urban areas. The main topics
of the course include an introduction to climate neutrality, roles of stakeholders, energy efficiency in urban planning and
construction, green solutions for transportation and transport infrastructure, waste management and circular economy,
monitoring and evaluation of implemented solutions, political and managerial frameworks, financing climate neutrality
projects, international best practices.

                   101148374 U_CAN - Part B - Page 3 of 27
1. Excellence #@REL-EVA-RE@#    Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)3060237 - 25/04/2024

1.1. Objectives #@PRJ-OBJ-PO@#

The EU mission "Climate-Neutral and Smart Cities" (CNSC) aims to achieve key EU policy priorities derived from
the European Green Deal (EGD). A core ambition of the EGD mission is to facilitate a Europe-wide transition to
climate neutrality by 2050. This necessitates a cross-sectoral approach to create synergies between cities already
committed to achieving climate neutrality by 2030 and those aiming for the same by 2050. Despite Ukraine's current
focus on delivering humanitarian and essential services due to ongoing conflict, the government expressed its intent
to embrace the EGD in 2020 along with several Ukrainian municipalities who have voiced their willingness to set
climate neutrality targets. In response to this imperative, the vision of the Project `U_CAN Ukraine towards
Carbon Neutrality' is to seamlessly integrate Ukrainian cities into the collective vision of the EGD by bonding
them with best-practice European counterparts - namely selected CNSC mission cities - through the usage of
advanced tools and methods for institutional networking, knowledge transfer and innovation management
(See Figure 1).

                                                             Figure 1: U_CAN Vision

In alignment with the vision outlined, U_CAN will enable the collaboration-minded Ukrainian cities to align their
strategies and standards in accordance with EU climate policies. Following the successful execution of the project,
the participating Ukrainian cities will have well-defined, context-specific climate strategies that effectively foster
climate neutrality efforts. This transformation would be achieved through a targeted support of the Ukrainian city
administrations´ efforts to collaborate and engage with their European counterparts. As a result of the facilitated
interaction with CNSC mission cities, the Ukrainian cities will have improved their capacities in the realm of climate
mitigation and be able to carry out local-specific plans and activities.

The mission encompasses five key objectives:
Objective 1: to support Ukrainian cities that are willing to transition to climate neutrality by developing context-
specific climate strategies. The envisioned outcome is a core group / network of pioneering Ukrainian stakeholders
who require support in building capacities to address climate neutrality.
Objective 2: to identify key sectors in Ukraine for climate transition. This includes identifying critical sectors that
require transitioning including industrial decarbonisation, energy transition, nature-based solutions, etc. Outcomes
envisioned here are recommendations, standards and best practices co-developed with EU experts.
Objective 3: to facilitate CNSC twinning activities, fostering the exchange of best practices in climate action. This
facilitation includes connecting CNSC Mission cities as pioneering examples and mentors for Ukrainian cities to
achieve climate neutrality. This leadership will leverage the expertise and success stories of established CNSC cities
to inspire and support their Ukrainian counterparts. The envisioned outcomes are long-term partnerships between
CNSC Mission cities and partner Ukrainian cities.
Objective 4: to facilitate local Pilot initiatives as indication of on-ground efforts demonstrating feasibility and
benefits of climate-neutral measures. This will also propagate tangible efforts in terms of replication and knowledge
exchange between Ukrainian partner cities and other cities in Ukraine. Outcomes envisioned here are localised pilot
implementation plans.
Objective 5: to facilitate awareness-building among a wider group of Ukrainian cities. This will ensure the
dissemination and exploitation of project results for long-term impacts on Ukraine's alignment with the EGD.
Outcome envisioned here is exploitation of the project activities to pan-Ukraine CNSC initiatives. #§PRJ-OBJ-PO§#

U_CAN Measures1.2. Coordination and/or support measures and methodolAosgsoycia#t@edCOwNith-MdoEcTu-mCMen@t R#e#f@. ACrOesM(2-0P2L4E)-3C0P6@02#37 - 25/04/2024

Being one of the biggest countries in Europe and currently subject to the Russian aggression, Ukraine (UA) faces
several challenges in catching up with the EU's climate ambitions. The environmental and infrastructural
deterioration caused by the ongoing war still amplifies Ukraine's pre-existing climate-related issues encompassing
energy-inefficient housing and transportation, air and water pollution, resource-intensive manufacturing,
unsustainable agricultural practices leading to land degradation, and various other challenges. Despite Ukraine's
active efforts to implement significant changes in environmental conservation, energy efficiency, and the promotion
of renewable energy, in line with the EU-UA Association Agreement, the list of reforms necessary for European
integration in these domains remains extensive. Specifically, in the realm of environment, the European Commission
recently evaluated Ukraine's alignment with EU standards as being in the early stages of preparation. In this regard,
the approach to achieving climate neutrality by 2050 in Europe requires holistic and transformative strategies from
Ukraine which can be achieved by establishing a comprehensive green cooperation between the expert partners in
the EU and the collaboration-minded partners in Ukraine.

The project provides a model approach and pathway for Ukraine's cities towards the EDG. U_CAN has been
developed together with the Ukrainian partners through several communications, interactions and consortium
meetings over the past months. All project measures are the result of a close cooperation with local city stakeholders
and representatives. Thus, U_CAN is able to strategically address the critical knowledge and expertise gaps in the
efforts of Ukrainian cities reaching for climate neutrality. The project aims to bridge these gaps and compensate
shortcomings in local capacity by targeted twinning and partnership-building with EU experts. U_CAN also
recognises the pressing need to enhance Ukraine's development standards, especially in reconstruction and green
infrastructure, as a prerequisite for the alignment with EU climate goals. Lastly, the project actively promotes
knowledge dissemination and replication, creating exemplary models for other Ukrainian cities, as a crucial condition
for scaling up climate practices.

U_CAN provides five measures to catalyse Ukraine's transition towards climate neutrality. Upon the initial outline
of specific climate challenges and key sectors for climate mitigation in the country, a roster of appropriate EU expert
partners will be composed to support technical capacity building for the selected Ukrainian cities, based on their
specified challenges. From the partnering cities in the EU, the Ukrainian partner cities will gain direct insight and
experience how to become a CNSC Mission city. Parallel to the CNSC-Ukrainian cities twinning, the EU-side
technical experts will co-develop with their Ukrainian counterparts relevant standard guidelines, frameworks, tools
and technologies to build the capacities of the Ukrainian stakeholders involved in climate mitigation efforts. Re-
enforcing the developed knowledge and the twinning exercise, local pilot use cases will be initiated in the Ukraine
to show the feasibility of small-scale climate-mitigating activities. These pilot showcases demonstrate how best
practices can be replicated and shared with other cities in Ukraine. The CNSC-supported pilot initiatives together
with tailored communication, dissemination and exploitation measures will exemplify Ukraine's successful efforts
towards climate neutrality at European and international level. Figure 2 illustrates the conceptual model including
the measures in line with the U_CAN objectives.

                                           Figure 2: Planned measures towards the U_CAN vision

                                                   101148374 U_CAN - Part B - Page 5 of 27
The five objectives developed by the U_CAN consortium are descArsibsoecdiaatesdfwoitlhlodwocsu:ment Ref. Ares(2024)3060237 - 25/04/2024

    (i) Collaboration-minded UA cities selection

Each of the selected Ukrainian cities offers a unique perspective and potential for contributing to the project vision.
Their diverse characteristics and regional influence can collectively drive the transition to climate neutrality across
Ukraine. The justifications for selection of the following eight Ukrainian cities in U_CAN are:

     Lviv, as a follower city in the Sustainable energy Positive & zero cARbon CommunitieS (SPARCS) project,
         is actively learning from European lighthouse cities like already established partner Leipzig, with its Positive
         Energy District pilot focusing on energy-efficient solutions and renewables.

     Kyiv, as the capital and largest city of Ukraine, holds significant strategic importance. Initiatives or projects
         implemented in Kyiv have the potential for broader impact due to the city's central role in the country's
         political, economic, and cultural landscape. Kyiv is home to many government institutions, making it easier
         to engage with policymakers and officials. This can be crucial for projects that require regulatory approvals
         or changes in government policies.

     Zhytomyr is the first city in Eastern Europe to sign a political declaration on the transition to 100%
         renewable energy sources by 2050. In 2022, the city of Zhytomyr joined the European initiative "Agreement
         of Mayors-East" and made a unilateral commitment to achieve climate neutrality by 2050. The city has
         defined the goals of decarbonisation and climate neutrality, which are enshrined in strategic and operational
         documents. In 2021, Zhytomyr achieved a 36% reduction of CO2 emissions compared to 2010.

     Khmelnytskyi is one of the Ukrainian cities to have joined the EBRD Green Cities programme and plans a
         more liveable urban space for its large inflows of internally displaced people due to the war, putting strain
         on the city.

     Ivano-Frankivsk has a very ambitious goal to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 40% before 2030 and
         push forward its Sustainable Energy Action Plan as a strategy that will synchronise the climate-neutral city
         development with CNSC mission. With the support of EBRD and Nordic Environment Finance Corporation
         (NEFCO) the city is already implementing renewable energy projects.

     Vinnytsia is the first Ukrainian territorial community that declared a local Green Deal and adopted the
         roadmap of its implementation till 2030. In 2015, Vinnytsia was the first city in Ukraine to pass the
         certification and receive European Energy Award. In 2019 Vinnytsia also received a European award in the
         field of sustainable urban mobility and clean transport CIVITAS 2019.

     Kharkiv as Ukraine's second-largest city and an educational centre takes the role of knowledge sharing in
         the realm of climate neutrality.

     Kherson's primary involvement is in coordinating exploitation activities for transitioning Ukraine's heating
         landscape to biofuels and contributing to the recovery and development of conflict-affected regions.

Lviv, Kyiv, Zhytomyr, Khmelnytskyi, Ivano-Frankivsk and Vinnytsia are chosen for pilot initiatives due to their
geographical distance from conflict zones, minimising security risks and ensuring a stable environment for project
implementation. Conversely, the cities of Kherson and Kharkiv are located in eastern and southern Ukraine, regions
that have been directly affected by the Russian aggression. Their proximity to conflict zones positions them as crucial
hubs for knowledge and exploitation facilitators and for reconstruction efforts.

    (ii) EU CNSC Mission cities and technical stakeholder formation

U_CAN´s concept model embraces the specific challenges and expertise in partner Ukrainian cities by establishing
tailored coordination and support partnerships with EU expert partners. The network of EU experts has been formed
based on the expertise demanded bottom-up by Ukrainian municipalities and project partners to meet the outcomes
of the call for associating Ukrainian cities with the CNSC mission. This includes urban and policy expertise, climate
and sustainability specialists, international cooperation and networking, technology proficiency, research and data
analysis, stakeholder engagement and communication, capacity building and training, project management and
coordination, regulatory knowledge, and interdisciplinary approaches especially in securing funding sources. These
areas of expertise collectively enable the consortium to contribute to the climate-neutral development of Ukrainian
cities. Figure 3 illustrates the regions of the Project partners within U_CAN.

                                                   101148374 U_CAN - Part B - Page 6 of 27
