The partners associated encompass a diverse range of stakeholders with specific purposes. U_CAN brings together
Europe-wide expertise across various sectors (CNSC Mission cities, NGOs, industry, academia, etc.,) to support
Ukrainian target groups (city authorities, NGOs, research groups, network associations) in identifying critical areas
for climate strategies and enhancing the capacities of these target groups and beyond them. The project is designed
to engage not only the 21 Project partners from EU and Ukraine, but also other interested Network partners who will
actively promote the cause of the project.

    a) Project partners: These include Dresden city as CNSC Mission city, European research institutions with
    project specific technical expertise, organisations with working experience in Ukraine's reconstruction efforts,
    standardisation, citizen science and clean energy projects; and nine partners from seven Ukrainian cities of Kyiv,
    Lviv, Zhytomyr, Khmelnytskyi, Ivano Frankivsk, Vinnytsia, Kharkiv and Kherson See Figure 3(b).
    b) Working Network Partners: It is planned to establish a Network Partners Board, which will include
    European Policy networks, such as representatives of EuroCities, ICLEI, NetZeroCities and CrAFt (Creating
    Actionable Futures) as part of the New European Bauhaus (NEB) initiative. The Board will also include other
    CNSC mission cities that are interested in engaging in twinning activities with Ukrainian project partners and
    also have previous partnerships with them, including Leipzig, Aarhus, Wroclaw, Bratislava, etc. The Network
    partners group also involves Ministries, local municipalities of Ukraine and other associations who are keen to
    closely participate with the Project partners. These partners have expressed their interest by the means of Letters
    of Interest and other written form of communication. The Network Partners Board is designed to expand after
    project initiation to involve further desirable stakeholders. Task 3.4 of the proposal is exclusively planned to
    engage with the European policy networks and will be carried out throughout the project timeframe.
    (iii) CNSC twinning and Use cases development
The CNSC Mission city of Dresden as a key project partner - along with other CNSC Mission cities as Network
Partners - is the keystone to this measure to build the capacities in Ukrainian cities to develop and implement
effective climate mitigation strategies. By developing specific use cases in detail, the measure will create a model for
the transfer of in-depth knowledge, expertise, and best practices from experienced CNSC Mission cities like Dresden
to their Ukrainian counterparts. It also facilitates the alignment of local efforts with broader European climate goals,
by nine use cases development for the cities of Lviv, Kyiv, Zhytomyr, Khmelnytskyi, Ivano-Frankivsk and Vinnytsia
to address localised climate challenges, key sectors and opportunities, allowing for tailored strategies in alignment
with common climate goals. This synergy-building process will not only strengthen the cohesion among Ukrainian
cities but also accelerate the collective progress towards climate-neutrality.
The use cases envisioned to be developed with support from the CNSC Mission cities will address and explore
various avenues to climate neutrality efforts. Use cases suggested by each Ukrainian pilot partner are as follows:

The first use case of Lviv will focus on creating a Positive Energy District (PED) in the city, aiming to improve
energy efficiency, reduce greenhouse gas emissions, and promote renewable energy solutions. This use case
envisions implementing various energy-efficient measures, including district heating upgrades, renewable energy
integration, and sustainable urban planning, ultimately serving as a model for other cities pursuing climate neutrality.
A PED also comes under the scope of the Work Programme 2023 of the Climate-Neutral and Smart Cities Mission.
The second use case involves establishing a Bureau for EU Integration and an Office of Lviv in Brussels to harmonise
EU integration processes at the municipal level and provide climate-neutral city development practices, with the aim
of testing, scaling, and advocating these practices for broader adoption among Ukrainian cities through partnerships
with CNSC cities and EU institutions.

                                                   101148374 U_CAN - Part B - Page 7 of 27
The use case of Kyiv involves the development of recommendatioAnsssotociaatdeddwtoiththdoecGumeennetrRaelf.PAlarens(o20f2t4h)e30c6i0ty23r7e-la2t5e/0d4/t2o024
the development of underground space, with the aim of increasing the use of the subway as a sustainable mobility
mode and reducing greenhouse gas emissions from transport. This measure includes assessing the emissions
reduction potential, planning the underground space with climate considerations, determining appropriate routes, and
transferring recommendations for integration into the city's master plan, with the potential for replication in other
Ukrainian cities with significant underground construction projects.

The first use case of Zhytomyr involves the development of floating solar power stations on water reservoirs, aiming
to generate clean energy for the city's utility company and promote the use of renewable energy sources, aligning
with the city's strategic goal of achieving European energy efficiency standards. The second use case focuses on
urban gardening, revitalising a former municipal plant nursery into a multifunctional space for urban gardening and
knowledge sharing, with plans to collaborate with CNSC mission cities to adapt and share best practices in urban
greenery development.

The first use case of Khmelnytskyi involves disseminating practical knowledge on urban traffic systems in the
context of emissions reduction in cities working towards achieving climate neutrality. This will engage key
stakeholders involved in traffic management and local government from various Ukrainian cities and facilitate
knowledge on existing traffic systems, environmental impact of traffic emissions and required policy changes by
local government. The second use case, "Center for the Support of Environmental Law" is designed to facilitate the
implementation of the EGD in Khmelnytskyi by disseminating information, offering legal consultations and training,
and advocating for improved legislation and regulations related to EU integration and the CNSC mission in the city.

The use case of Ivano Frankivsk involves the development and testing of a solar power station installation on a
public building in collaboration with city council representatives, NGOs, citizens, and businesses. This initiative aims
to verify the effectiveness of renewable energy source integration in the city, monitor energy generation, raise
awareness about renewable energy source integration among businesses and citizens, and co-develop policy
recommendations for scaling up renewable energy source integration, specifically PV installations, in the city.

The use case of Vinnytsia involves strategy development for restoration of urban biodiversity. It will also develop a
concept for revitalisation of the Tiazhylivka River which is one of the biggest tributaries of Southern Buh in
Vinnytsia.

    (iv) Pilot implementation

Pilot implementation within the scope of U_CAN, emerges as a response to key challenges identified and use cases
developed in the process of CNSC twinning with the six pilot cities in Ukraine. Pilot implementation as a measure,
despite not being stated explicitly in the call description, has been meticulously crafted through an iterative process
of extensive communication and collaborative meetings involving partners from CNSC Mission cities (Dresden and
Leipzig), the Ukrainian counterparts (Lviv, Kyiv, Zhytomyr, Khmelnytskyi, Ivano-Frankivsk and Vinnytsia) and
other facilitating Project partners. Drawing from diverse expertise and perspectives, these consultations have
highlighted the need for implementation of climate neutral strategies tailored to each pilot city. The pilot
implementation will foster a dynamic exchange among the CNSC `twins' and serve as practical demonstrations of
the concrete outcomes. They will effectively illustrate how cross-border collaboration and knowledge exchange
within the twinning framework can contribute to tangible progress toward climate neutrality. More importantly, they
will serve as exemplary models for other Ukrainian cities, facilitating effective knowledge dissemination and
replication of climate mitigation strategies within the country.

    (v) Adaptation of standards, policies and technologies in Ukraine

The measures of adapting standards, policies, and technologies in Ukrainian cities, is imperative to address pressing
climate challenges and achieve climate neutrality. Ukraine faces a critical need to enhance its building standards,
particularly in the context of reconstruction and green infrastructure. Current national standards may not align
adequately with climate goals, making it essential to develop updated frameworks that encourage green and carbon
neutral practices and to urgently integrate them in its cities. Assessment of current standards and the integration of
international and European best practices foster innovation and efficiency in addressing the foundational aspect of
climate neutrality.

                                                   101148374 U_CAN - Part B - Page 8 of 27
Significance of envisioned results:            Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)3060237 - 25/04/2024

1. The selection of Ukrainian cities will lead to a set of cities becoming an integral part of the CNSC Mission,

and accelerate the transition to climate-neutrality across the country.

2. Through CNSC twinning and knowledge exchange, Ukrainian cities will develop effective climate mitigation

strategies, aligning local efforts with broader European climate goals.

3. Pilot implementations will serve as exemplary models, fostering knowledge dissemination and replication of

climate mitigation strategies across Ukraine.

4. Integrating standards, policies, technologies and pilot success stories will enhance climate actions, building

standards; promote green and carbon-neutral practices in Ukrainian cities.

These outcomes will significantly contribute to Ukraine's urban infrastructure resilience, climate targets and

environmental sustainability, aligning with EU policies and international commitments.

Overall Communication and Dissemination measures
Communication, dissemination, and exploitation measures are pivotal in U_CAN, synergising with the five specific
measures to drive Ukraine's climate-neutrality transition. In the selection of Ukrainian cities like Lviv, Kyiv,
Zhytomyr, Khmelnytskyi, Ivano-Frankivsk and Vinnytsia, effective communication informs their roles as pilot cities,
fostering cooperation and alignment with project objectives. Dissemination channels facilitate transparency and
commitment while exploiting their unique strengths. For EU CNSC Mission Cities and technical stakeholders,
transparent communication facilitates knowledge exchange, with these cities sharing expertise and best practices
with Ukrainian counterparts. Exploitation maximises knowledge transfer, bridging gaps in Ukraine's climate efforts.
In CNSC twinning and use cases development, communication ensures the active sharing of expertise, while
dissemination channels promote replication of successful strategies. Pilot implementation relies on communication
to coordinate and inform stakeholders about progress and outcomes, with dissemination spreading results and lessons.
Finally, the integration of standards, policies, and technologies leverages communication to engage stakeholders in
implementing new standards and practices. Dissemination shares information and best practices, with exploitation
and ensures practical application in Ukrainian cities. Collectively, these measures and communication strategies
empower Ukraine to align with EU climate goals, fostering impactful climate initiatives.

Open Science Practices
U_CAN considers the principles of Open Science throughout all of its methodological parameters. In line with EU
Open Science Policy, the project seeks to contribute to greater knowledge generation. In order to effectively
disseminate and exploit the knowledge generated during and after the project, the following activities are assumed in
line with the open science and FAIR principles:

  Publications: Academic dissemination of the empirical activities, findings and outcomes of the project will
       be achieved through open-access publications. These will be undertaken by the research partners of the
       consortium in a collaborative manner, emphasising interdisciplinary co-authorship. In this way, the project
       guarantees an interdisciplinary and collaborative approach across all of its relevant publications.

  Webinars: Running alongside the project actions, a series of webinars, openly accessible through the U_CAN
       website will be produced to continuously present project methods and learning and gain insights from project-
       external experts, e.g. Network partners group. This will serve as professional learning content for replication
       and further development of the project.

   Website: A website, connected to or established within https://netzerocities.eu/ will be launched with
       systematic information on project progress, to encourage a more transparent communication of the U_CAN
       initiatives.

  Interdisciplinary / multi-level knowledge: The structure of U_CAN requires at its core the involvement of
       all relevant stakeholders in the co-creative process. In this sense, open exchange and knowledge sharing among
       different disciplines and participation is essential for the success of the implementation phase.

Research Data Management
All collected data are stored in a centralised database that is the basis of the platform that will be generated within
the scope of the project. The project will make the data publicly available, and, therefore, support the open-data
initiatives. It will follow the FAIR data paradigm, providing an open API that will give full access to the generated
data. All the collected information will be analysed as part of the project's procedures and visualisations of the
analysed information will be available on the dedicated dashboard. The plan of the project is to have the data open
during and after the end of the project using existing tools such as Zenodo as repository for any research data. A
detailed data management plan will be provided and distributed after the project initiation.

2. Impact                               Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)3060237 - 25/04/2024

2.1. Project's pathways towards impact

U_CAN's pathway towards impact recognises the unique opportunity arising from the convergence of Ukraine's
post-conflict reconstruction needs and the imperative of climate neutrality. The project implements a series of
activities aimed at preparing Ukrainian cities for active participation in European missions, ensuring their readiness
and alignment with mission objectives.

U_CAN plans five Activities envisioning Impacts towards the vision:

(i) Mapping of Ukrainian cities  Long-term Ukrainian City matches for Climate initiatives

The initial stage of U_CAN encompasses identification and commitment, of Ukrainian cities willing to commit to
climate neutrality. This foundational step signifies these cities' dedication to aligning with EU climate objectives. It
also serves as a pivotal step in identifying key sectors that require support, establishing a solid foundation for
meaningful participation in the CNSC Mission. Impact Scale / Significance: A core group stakeholders working
closely with the city administration in a minimum of eight Ukrainian cities (Lviv, Kyiv, Zhytomyr, Khmelnytskyi,
Ivano Frankivsk, Vinnytsia, Kharkiv and Kherson) as integral project partners. A minimum of 5 Ukrainian cities as
Network partners with interest in making/ continuing climate resolutions.

(ii) Supporting Ukrainian cities in the sectors identified  Strategic sectoral empowerment of cities in UA

U_CAN assembles a diverse group of EU stakeholders to coordinate and support identified cities and key sectors.
This pivotal step lays the foundation for the formation of the expert stakeholder network, which will play a crucial
role in providing the identified Ukrainian cities with essential expertise, guidance and practical collaborations. The
network significantly enhances the capacity of Ukrainian cities to pursue climate effectively, reflecting the project's
commitment to achieving impactful outcomes. Impact Scale / Significance: The expert stakeholder network includes
experienced EU experts in the fields of CNSC Mission (Dresden City); Research institutions with experience in
climate tool development, re-construction sector, citizen engagement, industrial decarbonisation etc (Technical
University Dresden - Germany, Wroclaw University of Science and Technology - Poland, Aarhus University -
Denamrk, University of L'Aquila - Italy, Joanneum Research GmbH - Austria); organizations with Ukraine-specific
knowledge in standards developments and practical reconstruction experience in Ukraine (DIN Deutsches Institut
für Normung - Germany, Technology Partners Foundation - Poland, Enviroplan Consultants & Engineers S.A -
Greece,); and communication and dissemination partners (European Citizen Science Association - Europe, Slovak
Foreign Policy Association - Slovakia, Technology Partners Foundation - Poland).

(iii) Twinning and teaming Ukraine with CNSC Mission cities  Long-term CNSC partnerships

U_CAN fosters twinning and teaming activities between Ukrainian cities and CNSC Mission cities enabling
Ukrainian cities to learn from their European counterparts, adopt green solutions, and integrate best practices. It
positions the identified cities as valued partners in the Cities Mission and underscores their commitment to
international cooperation. The twinning collaboration acts as a catalyst to enhancing the international profile of
Ukrainian cities, while also benefiting the CNSC Mission cities in transferring their experiences to other European
cities to collectively reach climate neutrality by 2050. U_CAN further enhances the capacity of local authorities in
Ukrainian cities. Alongside of CNSC twinning with the cities, U_CAN offers Capacity-building for the Ukrainian
partners to acquire the necessary skills to effectively address climate neutrality. This empowers them not only for the
CNSC Mission involvement but also for instigating practical change at the local level. Impact Scale/ Significance:
CNSC twins and teams for climate synergy building and exchanging climate experiences by CNSC Mission cities -
A minimum of 3 CNSC twins are formed, as a result of synergy building between CNSC Mission cities and Ukrainian
partner cities (Dresden-Khmelnytskyi, Leipzig-Lviv, and Bratislava-Zhytomyr). Active involvement of a minimum of
6 CNSC Mission overall to team with the six Pilot Ukrainian cities. Capacities of a minimum of 8 Ukrainian project
partners developed by equipping them with tools and knowledge - Capacity building tools like Indicators to assess
climate neutrality, Standards consolidation, guidelines for modernisation of technologies, best-practices for
industrial decarbonisation, tools for management of building rubble from war-effected regions, etc.

(iv) Facilitating Use case based Pilot initiatives  Feasible adaptation of climate actions in Ukraine

U_CAN develops Use cases from the twinning collaborations to launch pilot initiatives within the selected Ukrainian
cities. The localised pilot actions offer tangible demonstrations of climate-neutral strategies and serve as concrete

                                                  101148374 U_CAN - Part B - Page 10 of 27

showcase the feasibility and benefits of climate-neutral measures becoming strong candidates for Cities Mission
participation. Impact Scale / Significance: A minimum of six pioneering Ukrainian pilot cities lead localised

Ivano Frankivsk and Vinnytsia will serve as innovation hubs, leading the conception and execution of multi-sectoral

(v) Disseminating and Exploiting best practices, success stories and CNSC collaboration  Climate

U_CAN goes beyond technical capacity building and localised pilot actions to cultivate climate consciousness and
expertise among a wider group of Ukrainian stakeholders like the city administration, decision-makers, industries
and civil society. Awareness building and Training provides a holistic approach ensuring to serve a wide group of
target stakeholders. Furthermore, U_CAN actively engages with international and European networks and
institutions, including the Global Covenant of Mayors, NetZeroCities, the World Bank, and EBRD. This engagement
with international networks and Institutions not only grants access to additional resources and funding but also
extends the project's reach, increasing the visibility and global connectivity of Ukrainian cities. Impact Scale /

as leaders. Global partnerships like Mayors of Europe and Global Covenant of Mayors accelerate and create
meaningful change.
In summary, U_CAN's proposed activities, spanning from Ukrainian commitment, support by international partners,
CNSC collaboration, capacity-building, practical climate actions and dissemination by engaging wider target groups
and networks makes the path for integration of Ukrainian cities in the EGD and international commitments to climate-
neutrality.

External barriers that may influence U_CAN's impact and suggested mitigation

Project implementation during the Russian aggression in Ukraine, may potentially affect the proposed activities by
the Ukrainian partners. To mitigate the external risk of project disruption, U_CAN considers diversified partnerships,
developing a robust consortium of city administration, NGOs, universities and representatives of civil society from
various geo-political situations in Ukraine to ensure project resilience and adaptability. Furthermore, the Ukrainian
partners have experience in project implementation and synergy building amidst conflict conditions and therewith
soften external risks. Additionally, all key project activities can be seamlessly transitioned to digital formats if
necessary. Limited attention from target stakeholders may influence the support required due to ongoing hostilities
and dilute the project's impact. U_CAN intends to implement a proactive stakeholder engagement strategy, which
will include consistent meaningful communication, outreach efforts, and awareness campaigns centred around
stakeholder's specific needs and challenging circumstances to maintain stakeholder commitment.

Long-term project sustainability

The Project sustainability after U_CAN completion is an important element for the proposal. The project sets up on
working partnerships between CNSC and Ukrainian cities. These city partnerships will continue after the project,
ensuring its sustainability. The Consortium includes the annual conference "Central European Energy Conference"
hosted by the Slovak Foreign Policy Association in Bratislava, which ensures that the topics arising in the project
will continue to play a role in such events. Several Consortium members already started partnerships, ensuring the
long lasting effect of the projects impact. For instance, Dresden and Khmelnytskyi are already having a solidarity
partnership and can continue to further their exchanges through the CNSC Network. The Mayors Club of Ukraine
established started a common initiative with the alliance "Mayors of Europe" that will help to share positive
experiences long after the end of U_CAN. The issued topics related to climate neutrality on an urban scale will be
included in University course in Kiyv. Furthermore, the Ukrainian standardisation organisation and DIN have a long
history of cooperation in several twinning programmes. The standards developed within U_CAN also develop long
lasting impact. Last but not least the U_CAN Platform, including recordings of the courses held in the project, will
remain online and the results will be shared on well-known horizon platforms, providing training resources for further
CNSC courses after project timeline.

The dissemination, exploitation, and communication (DEC) activities of this project are described within WP6 and
WP7. They are firmly integrated into the project's overarching management structure. These DEC actions are pivotal
in ensuring the impact of U_CAN's outcomes by garnering the attention and engagement of key stakeholders. To
maximise the impacts of DEC actions, a two-pronged approach is adapted. Firstly, wider target groups are identified
with correlation to the spectrum of U_CAN activities and results. Secondly, communication tools and dissemination
channels are proposed. They are a result of a collaborative effort, involving consultations with all and leveraging the
collective expertise of all project partners in U_CAN. This approach ensures that the DEC strategy is tuned to align
with their activities and collectively optimise the project's outreach efforts, making DEC a central element of the
project's success.

Exploitation activities:

U_CAN plans multi-disciplinary exploitation measures that are designed to maximise the project's long-term impact
by conducting knowledge exchanges, fostering collaboration, and promoting the adoption of climate-neutral
strategies across Ukraine. Some of the exploitation measures included in the project are:

    - Networking Events: 1 Exchange event with Mayors of Europe, where Ukrainian and European Mayors
         network and exchange experiences with the representatives of UA and EU businesses & associations. 1
         Exchange event with the Global Covenant of Mayors - hybrid event in Ukraine involving experience
         exchanges between the Global Covenant of Mayors and the Ukrainian Mayors. These events will provide a
         compelling platform for CNSC Mission networking. Both the events together will include 75 mayors from
         different communities in Ukraine and EU.

    - Knowledge Dissemination Events: 2 Webinars targeting the industry stakeholders (min. 50 participants)
         and educating them about decarbonisation of industries. The project will organise workshops and seminars
         to disseminate knowledge and share project findings with a wider audience. These events will provide a
         platform for stakeholders to learn about successful strategies and best practices developed during the project.
         The Annual Central European Energy Conference will include U_CAN panels (for 2 years) to engage a
         wider audience (Practitioners, academic representatives, policy researchers, regional and national
         representatives) in U_CAN's case. Participation of U_CAN and financially supporting the conference in
         Bratislava is integral to the U_CAN project's mission of knowledge dissemination and exploitation. The
         conference's established reputation with many east European and Ukrainian partners and its strategic location
         in East-Central Europe, makes it an ideal platform for disseminating project findings and amplifying the
         project's impact. It would be the biggest dissemination event outside Ukraine and we chose it for its already
         well-established annual Central European Energy Conference (CEEC) activity. Every year, since 2007,
         CEEC discusses and assesses topical issues relating to energy and climate policy and invites EU
         representatives and ministries from central and eastern Europe. With U_CAN, we aim to engage our project
         partners and activities with diverse stakeholder experts and leverage the conference's networking
         opportunities in the east European Region by funding U_CAN panels in addition to the existing CEEC
         agenda.

    - Online Platforms: Apart from the U_CAN Platform, many project partners have well-established platforms
         (European Citizen Science Association, Mayors Club Ukraine) engaging a wide network of stakeholders
         and audience. U_CAN findings will be actively promoted on such channels to leverage any networking
         opportunities with relevant stakeholders. These platforms will also include exploitation results of the project
         like Roadmaps, Policy recommendations, toolkits etc. and can empower the broader audience, aiming to
         influence policy decisions and promote the adoption of climate-neutral strategies.

    - Training Programs: Dedicated Climate neutrality in Ukraine webinar focusing on the city authorities (max
         25 participants) will be conducted in Kyiv to ensure adaptation of recommended policies, climate initiatives,
         best practices and standards are adapted to the capital city's implementation plan.

                                                  101148374 U_CAN - Part B - Page 13 of 27
- Pan-Ukraine Initiatives: The project aims to catalyse laArgsseorc-isacteadlewitchlidmocautmeenint Riteiaf.tAivreess(2a0c24ro)3s0s60U23k7ra- i2n5e/04b/2y024
    supporting the expansion of successful initiatives beyond the project's pilot cities to create a broader, pan-
    Ukraine impact. This will be initiated by 6 site-visits to pilot districts by Ukrainian project partners as well
    as other stakeholders representing city administration, industry, civil society and research.

- Media Engagement: The project will engage with local media in Ukraine to raise awareness of its activities
    and outcomes. This will ensure that the project's successes are communicated widely, inspiring other cities
    and regions to take action.

- Small-scale projects will be initiated in the Kherson and Uman regions based on best practices and pilot
    initiation activities. These projects will be the first exploitation outputs in the realm of climate practices.

3. Quality and efficiency of the implementation #@QUA-LIT-QL@# #@WRK-PLA-WP@#

3.1. Work plan and resources

U_CAN's work plan has been envisioned in seven Work Packages to effectively meet the project's vision, objectives
and the outcomes of the Topic. Table 3.1a outlines the Work Packages and Figure 4 illustrates the correlation of
different Work packages in the project. Remarkably Ukrainian partners will lead three of the WPs, supporting the
bottom-up orientation of the project as a whole.

15/ MCU             80,000   support with development of the toolkit for local authorities to improve citizen
                             participation in climate governance (programming, architecture development,
                             Graphical User Interface), WP5, T5.5 (14k)

                             (1) Support for an online course for mayors in Ukraine on `Carbon-Neutral
                             Smart Cities' as part of WP6, Task6.2 (50k)
                             The course will be taught to Mayors of municipalities and members of their
                             administration, including but not limited to Heads of environmental protection
                             departments, and interested public organizations in Ukraine. The target for the
                             course is to involve 50 municipalities in Ukraine, with a total of 300 course
                             participants. The course will run over a period of 6 months and consist of 6
                             modules with around one hour of classes per week using a mix of pre-recorded
                             and live sessions. At the end of the online lectures, an on-site event will be held
                             for course participants.

                             (2) Support for a course for mayors on `Purposeful leadership and responsible
                             governance for the Mayor's in Ukraine as part of WP6, Task6.2 (30k)
                             The course is targeted at Mayors in Ukraine focusing on organizational
                             frameworks and the importance of governance from a leader's perspective. In
                             12 units over 6 months 50 participants will engage in strategy work,
                             relationships and communication with stakeholders. The course will equip
                             Mayors in Ukraine with the skills to ensure that U_CAN recommendations and
                             results will be implemented in their cities. The course programme comprises
                             blended content, along with two online seminars.

                             The budgeted costs for both courses include preparation of course content in
                             collaboration with U_CAN partners, preparation of recorded content (subtitles,
                             video editing), provision of a course platform, communication and technical
                             support for participants, translation services (course material, recorded video,
                             live translation) and costs for the final on-site/hybrid event.

                             Ukraine.

3.2. Capacity of participants and consortium as a whole #A@sCsoOciNat-eSdOwRit-hCdSo@cu#m#e@ntPRReJf.-MAreGsT(2-0P2M4)@30#60237 - 25/04/2024

The consortium assembled for U_CAN is the essence of excellence, versatility and resilience, making it the ideal
choice for this endeavour. Its collective expertise spans a wide spectrum of critical climate areas, ensuring a holistic
and comprehensive approach to climate neutrality. With 10 out of the 21 project partners being municipal bodies,
NGOs, universities and network associations from Ukraine, the consortium proudly showcases its collaboration. This
ensures an accurate Ukrainian representation, realistic and active engagement for knowledge transfer, and thus,
impactful climate dialogues. This contributes to added value to the project. The pre-existing partnerships of some
EU and UA members such as DIN-UA (in standardisation), LHD - Khmelnytskyi city council (in solidarity
partnership), ENV-UA (in infrastructure projects), SFPA-Ukraine (in dissemination and capacity building) ensure
a robust pathway towards the project's impact. Ukrainian project partners like the CIL, ZCC, ME IUD are all
established by their city councils and guarantee adoption of climate resolutions by their cities. The Project
Coordinator - Technische Universität Dresden and the key CNSC Project Partner - Landeshauptstadt Dresden are
established in the city of Dresden. Dresden city, also described as the "gateway to Central Europe", maintains close,
historically evolved relationships with the neighbouring countries and is engaged in an intensive exchange with both
Eastern and Western European cities. Dresden, along with the other interested CNSC Mission Cities of Bratislava
and Wroclaw share a common socialist heritage, with the Ukrainian city planning and built structures. The knowledge
transfer from these CNSC project and network partner cities will include the transition experience of the city since
the 90s and therefore, benefiting the Ukrainian cities.

4. Ethics self-assessment  Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)3060237 - 25/04/2024

Ethical dimension of the objectives, methodology and likely impact

The proposal is subject to the call HORIZON-MISS-2023-CIT-02 as a Coordination and Support action, including
twinning activities between European CNSC Mission cities and Ukrainian cities. Within the call, these Twinning and
teaming activities will include officials, researchers and CSOs for instance in training programmes and exchange
activities. Due to the ongoing conflict and related risks, Partners in Ukraine should be considered to be vulnerable
individuals or groups. Therefore, personalised data as well as the mere participation in the project can be interpreted
as the expression of political opinion and should be treated with special care and sensitivity, though accordance to
the GDPR is not yet mandatory in the country. It depends on the further military developments, how the frontlines
in some regions move throughout the time of the project. Regions and cities such as Kharkiv and Kherson are in
grave danger. Few hours before the deadline of this call, combats are taking place in the immediate surrounding of
these cities. Therefore there is an immediate need for support, as it is issued by the aim to rebuild Ukrainian cities.
However, the processing of personalised data and the involvement of individuals and groups of these regions may
increase their relevance for the Russian aggressors. Importantly, internally Displaced People are also included in all
the pilot and other partner cities forming a vulnerable group with special needs.

Furthermore, Ukraine is listed as a lower middle income country, making it necessary to assure that the involved
parties benefit from the planned actions.

Compliance with ethical principles and relevant legislations

The identified ethical and legal issues are treated with European legislations and ethical principles as a strict and
minimal precondition. Objectives of the activities, structural objectives and ongoing practices are subject to the
coordination of the project (WP1).

In addition some of the ethical issues, such as the inclusion of vulnerable Groups and their support, form an integral
part of the whole project and will be addressed in accordance to its down to earth, Citizen Science augmented Design.
Another ethical issue regarding the involvement of middle- and lower-income-countries cannot be avoided due to
the general focus of the call on twinning activities with Ukrainian cities. However, they will be continuously reflected
and assessed in Task 1.3 and will be issued in the consecutive development of the project. To ensure that the involved
parties in those countries benefit of the process, is guaranteed by the general design of specific measures based on
local circumstances and necessities in Ukraine.

The issue of data protection will be addressed by providing the infrastructure for communications and knowledge
transfer from European servers according to the European Data Governance Act and other legal instruments such as
the GDPR. Principles such as Privacy by default will guide the development of the knowledge platform and the
related data peer-to-peer exchange.

Profiling of participants will be strictly avoided while the processing and usage of previously collected personal data
will be reduced to a minimum.

SME owners/natural person beneficiaries without salary (Decision C(2020) 71151)

Type: unit costs

Units: days spent working on the action (rounded up or down to the nearest half-day)

Amount per unit (daily rate): calculated according to the following formula:
                  {EUR 5 080 / 18 days = 282,22}
                  multiplied by
                  {country-specific correction coefficient of the country where the beneficiary is established}
